```text
Still 77 for a long time now. It seems like the sensor_noise episode is taking really long. This has been stuck at 77 for quite a while. But the process is alive and the log is being modified. Let me verify one more time that it's actually running and not in some error state.

Actually, looking at it differently - episode 2 of sensor_noise was logged but the episode itself is taking a very long time. This might be because sensor_noise perturbations cause very long rollouts (the robot might be struggling due to noisy sensor readings, and the episode runs for the full max steps - potentially 300+ steps).


Each step is computationally expensive: pulling observations from LIBERO, running inference through the pi0_libero model on CUDA, collecting activations, and executing actions in the simulator. With 300+ steps and that heavy inference load, a single episode could easily take several minutes. I'll just wait and keep monitoring since the process is definitely still running.
```

The activation collection is running but taking longer than initially estimated due to sensor_noise episodes being much longer (max-step rollouts of 300+ steps each). Current status:

**Completed**: 3/6 types saved (camera_viewpoints, light_conditions, background_textures — 1100 steps each)
**In progress**: sensor_noise (3/25 episodes)
**Pending**: objects_layout, robot_initial_states

The process (PID 679866) is alive and healthy — no errors. However, sensor_noise episodes take 1-3 minutes each (vs ~20s for visual perturbation types), likely because noisy sensors cause the robot to run full-length episodes without completing the task. This means the remaining 3 types could take another 1-2 hours.

The collection will complete on its own. Once all 6 "Saved" lines appear in the log, the next step is to run the router training script:
```bash
bash /root/meta_brain_lora_routing/phase1/train_par_router_v2.sh
```
